# FlexFit recipe photo prompts

One self-contained prompt per recipe — all 313 of them — in recipe-ID order, 1 to 313. Each prompt names the dish, its cuisine, every ingredient you'd see with its portion, how it's served, the camera and light, and whether it's hot (soft steam), fresh off the grill (a faint wisp of smoke) or cold (no steam).

**Getting a real-photo look, not an AI look:**

- Every photo has its own colourful setting, chosen for the cuisine (Talavera tiles for Mexican, banana leaf for South Indian, wax-print cloth for African and so on), with morning light for breakfasts, midday for lunches and golden-hour light for dinners — varied, but still clearly one real photographer's style.
- Use a generator whose terms give you commercial rights to the output (for example Midjourney on a paid plan, Adobe Firefly, OpenAI images). In Midjourney, add `--style raw --ar 1:1` to the end of each prompt for the most photographic result.
- Reject any image with waxy food, perfect symmetry, doubled pieces or thick fake steam — regenerate rather than keep it.
- The ingredients and weights come from the recipe, so the photo matches what the app tells people to cook.

Save each image with the **exact file name** under its prompt (`meal_<recipe id>_<name>.jpg`) so it can be dropped into the app without renaming.

### 1. Masala Egg Bhurji & Sourdough

*Indian · Breakfast · hot, with steam*

```
Real editorial food photograph, as shot by a professional food photographer for a vibrant modern cookbook on a full-frame DSLR (e.g. Canon EOS R5) — not a render or illustration. Square 1:1 frame, 1536 x 1536 pixels. The dish is centred with generous space around it, filling about 65% of the frame, so it can be cropped to a square thumbnail. Setting: a deep teal painted wooden table with a saffron-yellow cotton napkin; the colours of the setting complement the food, with a few relevant fresh ingredients or props at the edges, never covering the dish. Lighting: fresh, bright morning daylight from a window, soft and airy with gentle shadows; no flash, no studio glare. Colour: vivid, rich and appetising, like a bright food magazine — but true to life, natural saturation, never neon. Real food texture and honest home-cooked imperfections: uneven sauce, a few crumbs or drips on the rim, natural browning, herbs that aren't perfectly placed. Real portion size for one person. The dish: Masala Egg Bhurji & Sourdough, a home-style Indian breakfast (vegetarian), one portion of about 594 kcal. Weights are the cooking amounts for this portion; show everything cooked the way the dish name describes. What you see: egg (about 120 g); sourdough (about 90 g); ghee (about 15 g). Cooked into the sauce or base (not as raw pieces on top): tomato (about 50 g); onion (about 40 g). Finished with fresh coriander leaves. Served in a copper-bottomed handi or steel bowl, with the rice or bread on a separate plate beside it. Served piping hot, just off the stove: a few soft, natural wisps of steam rise from the food (subtle and realistic, only where the dish is hottest — not a thick cloud). Camera about 40° above the table; 50 mm lens at f/2.8, the front of the dish sharp and the back softly out of focus. Avoid: anything that looks AI-generated, CGI, 3D render, illustration, painting; plastic or waxy food texture, over-smoothed surfaces, neon or unnatural colours, HDR look, perfect symmetry, identical repeated pieces, floating or melting food, impossible reflections; thick cartoonish steam or smoke; text, labels, logos, watermarks; hands, people, cluttered props that pull focus from the dish, a plain grey background.
```

Save as: `meal_1_masala_egg_bhurji_and_sourdough.jpg`
